Use when the user runs /design-sync or says \"sync my design system to Claude Design\".","argumentHint":"[<project hint, e.g. \"Acme DS\">]","builtin":true},{"name":"dataviz","description":"Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"update-config","description":"Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"verify","description":"Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"debug","description":"Enable debug logging for this session and help diagnose issues","argumentHint":"[issue description]","builtin":true},{"name":"code-review","description":"Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.","argumentHint":"[low|medium|high|xhigh|max|ultra] [--fix] [--comment] [<pr#>|<branch>|<path>]","aliases":["review"],"builtin":true},{"name":"simplify","description":"Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.","argumentHint":"[<target>]","builtin":true},{"name":"batch","description":"Research and plan a large-scale change, then execute it in parallel across 5–30 isolated worktree agents that each open a PR.","argumentHint":"<instruction>","builtin":true},{"name":"fewer-permission-prompts","description":"Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"doctor","description":"Health-check the user's Claude Code setup and fix issues: diagnose installation health — what the `claude doctor` terminal diagnostics cover — from local data (duplicate or leftover installs, PATH, unparseable settings files, broken or colliding agent definitions, skills whose frontmatter fails to parse); find unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins versus their context cost and disable dead weight; deduplicate local CLAUDE.md files against checked-in ones; trim checked-in CLAUDE.md files by cutting content a session could derive from the codebase (directory layouts, tech-stack lists, architecture overviews) while keeping gotchas, rationale, and non-standard conventions; migrate always-loaded CLAUDE.md guidance into lazy skills and nested CLAUDE.md files; flag slow hooks and context-heavy extensions; check the installed version is current; make auto mode the default permission mode; and pre-approve frequently denied read-only commands. Use when the user asks for a doctor run, checkup, audit, tune-up, or cleanup of their Claude Code setup or configuration.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["checkup"],"builtin":true},{"name":"loop","description":"Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Claude desktop app (also called the in-app browser, the browser pane, Claude's browser, or \"your own browser\"), whose tools are named mcp__Claude_Browser__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["built-in-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:chrome-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["chrome-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:computer-use","description":"Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["computer-use"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:deep-research","description":"Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":""},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docs","description":"docs (living docs people share, comment on and edit; use only when the user asks for one: names a doc, document, page, memo, spec, PRD, runbook or write-up, asks for somewhere to share or keep editing something, or says yes to your doc offer; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat (at most a one-line doc offer); a report, status update, recap or \"something I can send them\" with no form named → ask first: reply, doc or file?; tabs hold tables and live charts too; a pasted claude.ai/code/artifact/… link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; not HTML pages, apps or plain chat answers; a .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/PDF asked for by name → that format's skill): asked for one → no docs-connector instructions in context? call the docs connector's `guide` with topic.instructions first, then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. Documenting code means docstrings or repo docs, not a doc. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docs"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docx","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:import-memory","description":"Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["import-memory"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:morning","description":"Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["morning"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pdf","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pdf"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pptx","description":"Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pptx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:setup-writing-style","description":"Learns how the user writes from their own sent messages and docs, and builds a voice profile so future drafts sound like them instead of generic AI. The profile is saved as the my-writing-style skill. Use when the user asks to set up, learn, or capture their writing voice, or complains that drafts sound generic or unlike them and no my-writing-style profile exists. Only for drafting text the user will send as themselves, not for Claude's own replies. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["setup-writing-style"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:skill-creator","description":"Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["skill-creator"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:xlsx","description":"Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["xlsx"]}],"uuid":"2ef7998f-aadf-40dd-9bae-ea2771d02bf8","session_id":"9a4c96ab-bfa5-4654-b4ee-6068c2702ebb"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HzyVKXHCiMB8VgWE43cLK4","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';\n2\timport { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\n3\timport { resolve } from 'node:path';\n4\t\n5\tconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\n6\t\n7\tfunction gitLsFiles(patterns: string[]): string[] {\n8\t  const out = execFileSync('git', ['-C', REPO_ROOT, 'ls-files', ...patterns], {\n9\t    encoding: 'utf8',\n10\t    maxBuffer: 16 * 1024 * 1024,\n11\t  });\n12\t  return out.split('\\n').filter(Boolean).sort();\n13\t}\n14\t\n15\tdescribe('PostgreSQL DDL migration contract', () => {\n16\t  const migrationFiles = (): string[] =>\n17\t    gitLsFiles(['apps/*/src/migrations/*.ts', 'apps/*/src/database/migrations/*.ts']);\n18\t\n19\t  it('does not use unsupported ALTER TYPE IF EXISTS syntax', () => {\n20\t    const violations = migrationFiles().filter((relPath) => {\n21\t      const source = readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, relPath), 'utf8');\n22\t      return /\\bALTER\\s+TYPE\\s+IF\\s+EXISTS\\b/i.test(source);\n23\t    });\n24\t\n25\t    expect(violations).toEqual([]);\n26\t  });\n27\t\n28\t  it('does not transfer sequence ownership with schema-wide relkind sweeps', () => {\n29\t    const violations = migrationFiles().filter((relPath) => {\n30\t      const source = readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, relPath), 'utf8');\n31\t      const changesSequenceOwner = /\\bALTER\\s+SEQUENCE\\b[\\s\\S]*?\\bOWNER\\s+TO\\b/i.test(source);\n32\t      const sweepsSchemaSequences = /\\brelkind\\s*=\\s*'S'\\b/i.test(source);\n33\t      const scopesByOwnedDependency = /\\bJOIN\\s+pg_depend\\b/i.test(source);\n34\t\n35\t      return changesSequenceOwner && sweepsSchemaSequences && !scopesByOwnedDependency;\n36\t    });\n37\t\n38\t    expect(violations).toEqual([]);\n39\t  });\n40\t\n41\t  it('scopes config sequence ownership repair to sequences owned by config domain tables (or migration archived post-Faz-6 reset)', () => {\n42\t    // ADR-030 day-one baseline reset (Faz 6) archived this config-service\n43\t    // migration along with the rest of the pre-reset chain. The config\n44\t    // schema's sequence ownership is now declared structurally in the\n45\t    // baseline migration (1800000000000-Baseline.ts) — there is no\n46\t    // \"ownership repair\" code path because the baseline establishes the\n47\t    // canonical state in a single atomic migration. If the file is\n48\t    // gone, the regression class is closed by construction.\n49\t    const fs = require('node:fs') as typeof import('node:fs');\n50\t    const target = resolve(\n51\t      REPO_ROOT,\n52\t      'apps/config-service/src/database/migrations/1789100000000-OwnConfigTablesByConfigService.ts',\n53\t    );\n54\t    if (!fs.existsSync(target)) {\n55\t      expect(true).toBe(true);\n56\t      return;\n57\t    }\n58\t    const source = readFileSync(target, 'utf8');\n59\t    const sequenceLoop = source.match(/\\bFOR\\s+seq\\s+IN[\\s\\S]*?\\bLOOP\\b/i)?.[0] ?? '';\n60\t    expect(sequenceLoop).toContain('JOIN pg_depend dep');\n61\t    expect(sequenceLoop).toContain(\n62\t      \"owning_rel.relname IN ('configurations', 'configuration_history')\",\n63\t    );\n64\t    expect(sequenceLoop).not.toMatch(/\\bFROM\\s+pg_class\\s+c\\b[\\s\\S]*\\bAND\\s+c\\.relkind\\s*=\\s*'S'/i);\n65\t  });\n66\t});\n67\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9a4c96ab-bfa5-4654-b4ee-6068c2702ebb","uuid":"90260fce-f4c3-4c65-b10d-5637ce909f32","timestamp":"2026-09-19T23:36:16.329Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-766ab32e8788/tests/invariants/postgres-ddl-contract.spec.ts","content":"import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';\nimport { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\nimport { resolve } from 'node:path';\n\nconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\n\nfunction gitLsFiles(patterns: string[]): string[] {\n  const out = execFileSync('git', ['-C', REPO_ROOT, 'ls-files', ...patterns], {\n    encoding: 'utf8',\n    maxBuffer: 16 * 1024 * 1024,\n  });\n  return out.split('\\n').filter(Boolean).sort();\n}\n\ndescribe('PostgreSQL DDL migration contract', () => {\n  const migrationFiles = (): string[] =>\n    gitLsFiles(['apps/*/src/migrations/*.ts', 'apps/*/src/database/migrations/*.ts']);\n\n  it('does not use unsupported ALTER TYPE IF EXISTS syntax', () => {\n    const violations = migrationFiles().filter((relPath) => {\n      const source = readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, relPath), 'utf8');\n      return /\\bALTER\\s+TYPE\\s+IF\\s+EXISTS\\b/i.test(source);\n    });\n\n    expect(violations).toEqual([]);\n  });\n\n  it('does not transfer sequence ownership with schema-wide relkind sweeps', () => {\n    const violations = migrationFiles().filter((relPath) => {\n      const source = readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, relPath), 'utf8');\n      const changesSequenceOwner = /\\bALTER\\s+SEQUENCE\\b[\\s\\S]*?\\bOWNER\\s+TO\\b/i.test(source);\n      const sweepsSchemaSequences = /\\brelkind\\s*=\\s*'S'\\b/i.test(source);\n      const scopesByOwnedDependency = /\\bJOIN\\s+pg_depend\\b/i.test(source);\n\n      return changesSequenceOwner && sweepsSchemaSequences && !scopesByOwnedDependency;\n    });\n\n    expect(violations).toEqual([]);\n  });\n\n  it('scopes config sequence ownership repair to sequences owned by config domain tables (or migration archived post-Faz-6 reset)', () => {\n    // ADR-030 day-one baseline reset (Faz 6) archived this config-service\n    // migration along with the rest of the pre-reset chain. The config\n    // schema's sequence ownership is now declared structurally in the\n    // baseline migration (1800000000000-Baseline.ts) — there is no\n    // \"ownership repair\" code path because the baseline establishes the\n    // canonical state in a single atomic migration. If the file is\n    // gone, the regression class is closed by construction.\n    const fs = require('node:fs') as typeof import('node:fs');\n    const target = resolve(\n      REPO_ROOT,\n      'apps/config-service/src/database/migrations/1789100000000-OwnConfigTablesByConfigService.ts',\n    );\n    if (!fs.existsSync(target)) {\n      expect(true).toBe(true);\n      return;\n    }\n    const source = readFileSync(target, 'utf8');\n    const sequenceLoop = source.match(/\\bFOR\\s+seq\\s+IN[\\s\\S]*?\\bLOOP\\b/i)?.[0] ?? '';\n    expect(sequenceLoop).toContain('JOIN pg_depend dep');\n    expect(sequenceLoop).toContain(\n      \"owning_rel.relname IN ('configurations', 'configuration_history')\",\n    );\n    expect(sequenceLoop).not.toMatch(/\\bFROM\\s+pg_class\\s+c\\b[\\s\\S]*\\bAND\\s+c\\.relkind\\s*=\\s*'S'/i);\n  });\n});\n","numLines":67,"startLine":1,"totalLines":67}}}

{ createMockDataSource } from '@aquaculture/testing';\nimport type { QueryRunner } from 'typeorm';\n\nimport { EnforcePermanentConfigurationErasure1807300000000 } from '../1807300000000-EnforcePermanentConfigurationErasure';\n\ndescribe('EnforcePermanentConfigurationErasure1807300000000', () => {\n  let queryRunner: jest.Mocked<QueryRunner>;\n\n  beforeEach(() => {\n    ({ mockQueryRunner: queryRunner } = createMockDataSource());\n    queryRunner.query.mockResolvedValue(undefined);\n  });\n\n  it('uses explicit RLS authority and deletes only tenants with non-dry config proofs', async () => {\n    await new EnforcePermanentConfigurationErasure1807300000000().up(queryRunner);\n\n    const sql = queryRunner.query.mock.calls.map(([statement]) => String(statement)).join('\\n');\n    expect(sql).toContain(\"set_config('app.bypass_rls', 'on', true)\");\n    expect(sql).toContain(\"current_setting('app.bypass_rls', true) IS DISTINCT FROM 'on'\");\n    expect(sql).toContain('DELETE FROM \"config\".\"configuration_history\"');\n    expect(sql).toContain('DELETE FROM \"config\".\"configurations\"');\n    expect(sql).toContain('proof.\"targetService\" = \\'config-service\\'');\n    expect(sql).toContain('proof.\"dryRun\" = false');\n    expect(sql).toContain('configuration.\"tenant_id\" = erased.tenant_id');\n  });\n\n  it('is deliberately forward-only because erased secrets cannot be restored', async () => {\n    await new EnforcePermanentConfigurationErasure1807300000000().down(queryRunner);\n\n    const sql = queryRunner.query.mock.calls.map(([statement]) => String(statement)).join('\\n');\n    expect(sql).toContain('cannot roll back historical config erasure residual repair');\n  });\n});\n","numLines":34,"startLine":1,"totalLines":34}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YATnMWCDJxNRpcRrcLkLXS","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport { MigrationInterface, QueryRunner } from 'typeorm';\n2\timport { MigrationLogger, pinSearchPath } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/database';\n3\t\n4\t/**\n5\t * RestoreConfigSchemaOwnerBoundary1807400000000\n6\t * ============================================================================\n7\t *\n8\t * `config` was the one schema on the platform whose RUNTIME LOGIN role owned it.\n9\t *\n10\t * Bootstrap stage 008 (\"Least-Privilege Runtime Boundary\") moves every schema's\n11\t * ownership to a NOLOGIN `<svc>_schema_owner`, reachable only through\n12\t * `GRANT <owner> TO db_migrate`, so a compromised service credential cannot drop\n13\t * or re-shape its own schema. `1800100000000-OwnConfigTablesByConfigService`\n14\t * then ran — service migrations apply after bootstrap — and reversed it with\n15\t * `ALTER SCHEMA config OWNER TO config_service`, asserting that ownership in its\n16\t * own `postCondition()`. Two gates in the same deploy pipeline required opposite\n17\t * states, and the later one won.\n18\t *\n19\t * Nothing noticed because the assertion that checks it\n20\t * (`schema-invariants.spec.ts` B.4) was never executed by any CI step until\n21\t * 2026-07-28, and when it was, it expected the pre-stage-008 model and had to be\n22\t * corrected first. `config` is what the corrected assertion found.\n23\t *\n24\t * ## Why only the SCHEMA moves back\n25\t *\n26\t * 1800100000000's stated reason is real: RLS can only be enabled by the table\n27\t * owner, and config-service installs its tenant RLS policy at boot when\n28\t * `isSchemaDdlOwnedByDbMigrate` is false. That needs ownership of\n29\t * `configurations` and `configuration_history` — it does NOT need ownership of\n30\t * the schema. Schema ownership additionally confers DROP SCHEMA and the right to\n31\t * reassign everything inside it, which is exactly the authority the boundary\n32\t * exists to withhold, and no part of the RLS path uses it.\n33\t *\n34\t * So the tables, types and sequences stay with `config_service` and the boot\n35\t * path is untouched; only the schema returns to the owner role, with\n36\t * `USAGE, CREATE` granted so the service can still create objects it needs.\n37\t *\n38\t * Skips cleanly when `config_schema_owner` does not exist — a database that has\n39\t * not run stage 008 has no boundary to restore, and inventing the role here\n40\t * would put ownership somewhere no grant points at.\n41\t */\n42\texport class RestoreConfigSchemaOwnerBoundary1807400000000 implements MigrationInterface {\n43\t  name = 'RestoreConfigSchemaOwnerBoundary1807400000000';\n44\t\n45\t  private readonly logger = new MigrationLogger(this.name);\n46\t\n47\t  public async up(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n48\t    await pinSearchPath(qr, 'config');\n49\t\n50\t    // One statement per query() call. A single string carrying the DO block AND\n51\t    // the SELECT is a MULTI-statement simple query: the driver returns one result\n52\t    // per statement, TypeORM hands back the LAST one's `rows` — which for a DO\n53\t    // block is absent — so `rows` arrived undefined and `rows[0]` threw, aborting\n54\t    // the whole config migration chain (and with it every bootstrap-from-scratch\n55\t    // service assertion).\n56\t    await qr.query(`\n57\t      DO $$\n58\t      BEGIN\n59\t        IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'config_schema_owner') THEN\n60\t          ALTER SCHEMA config OWNER TO config_schema_owner;\n61\t          -- The runtime role keeps everything it actually uses: USAGE to reach\n62\t          -- the schema, CREATE because the boot-time RLS/audit helpers may add\n63\t          -- objects. Neither confers DROP SCHEMA nor REASSIGN OWNED.\n64\t          GRANT USAGE, CREATE ON SCHEMA config TO config_service;\n65\t        END IF;\n66\t      END $$\n67\t    `);\n68\t\n69\t    const rows: Array<{ applied: boolean }> = await qr.query(\n70\t      `SELECT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'config_schema_owner') AS applied`,\n71\t    );\n72\t\n73\t    this.logger.log(\n74\t      rows[0]?.applied\n75\t        ? 'config schema ownership returned to config_schema_owner; tables stay with config_service for RLS.'\n76\t        : 'config_schema_owner absent (pre-stage-008 database) — schema ownership left as found.',\n77\t    );\n78\t  }\n79\t\n80\t  /**\n81\t   * BOTH facts, because each has its own failure mode: the schema must not be\n82\t   * owned by the login role (the boundary), and the tables must still be owned\n83\t   * by it (the RLS install). A future change that \"fixes\" one by breaking the\n84\t   * other fails here rather than at a service's cold start.\n85\t   */\n86\t  public async postCondition(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<boolean> {\n87\t    const rows: Array<{ ok: boolean }> = await qr.query(`\n88\t      SELECT (\n89\t        NOT EXISTS (\n90\t          SELECT 1\n91\t            FROM pg_namespace n\n92\t            JOIN pg_roles r ON r.oid = n.nspowner\n93\t           WHERE n.nspname = 'config'\n94\t             AND r.rolname = 'config_service'\n95\t             AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'config_schema_owner')\n96\t        )\n97\t        AND NOT EXISTS (\n98\t          SELECT 1\n99\t            FROM pg_class c\n100\t            JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = c.relnamespace\n101\t            JOIN pg_roles r ON r.oid = c.relowner\n102\t           WHERE n.nspname = 'config'\n103\t             AND c.relkind IN ('r', 'p')\n104\t             AND c.relname IN ('configurations', 'configuration_history')\n105\t             AND r.rolname <> 'config_service'\n106\t        )\n107\t      ) AS ok\n108\t    `);\n109\t    return rows[0]?.ok === true;\n110\t  }\n111\t\n112\t  public async down(_qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n113\t    this.logger.warn(\n114\t      'Down intentionally a no-op: handing a schema back to a login role would ' +\n115\t        're-open the privilege boundary this migration closed.',\n116\t    );\n117\t  }\n118\t}\n119\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9a4c96ab-bfa5-4654-b4ee-6068c2702ebb","uuid":"1c6ca976-e4db-4bee-b7be-061b111a56ca","timestamp":"2026-09-19T23:36:42.882Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-766ab32e8788/apps/config-service/src/database/migrations/1807400000000-RestoreConfigSchemaOwnerBoundary.ts","content":"import { MigrationInterface, QueryRunner } from 'typeorm';\nimport { MigrationLogger, pinSearchPath } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/database';\n\n/**\n * RestoreConfigSchemaOwnerBoundary1807400000000\n * ============================================================================\n *\n * `config` was the one schema on the platform whose RUNTIME LOGIN role owned it.\n *\n * Bootstrap stage 008 (\"Least-Privilege Runtime Boundary\") moves every schema's\n * ownership to a NOLOGIN `<svc>_schema_owner`, reachable only through\n * `GRANT <owner> TO db_migrate`, so a compromised service credential cannot drop\n * or re-shape its own schema. `1800100000000-OwnConfigTablesByConfigService`\n * then ran — service migrations apply after bootstrap — and reversed it with\n * `ALTER SCHEMA config OWNER TO config_service`, asserting that ownership in its\n * own `postCondition()`. Two gates in the same deploy pipeline required opposite\n * states, and the later one won.\n *\n * Nothing noticed because the assertion that checks it\n * (`schema-invariants.spec.ts` B.4) was never executed by any CI step until\n * 2026-07-28, and when it was, it expected the pre-stage-008 model and had to be\n * corrected first. `config` is what the corrected assertion found.\n *\n * ## Why only the SCHEMA moves back\n *\n * 1800100000000's stated reason is real: RLS can only be enabled by the table\n * owner, and config-service installs its tenant RLS policy at boot when\n * `isSchemaDdlOwnedByDbMigrate` is false. That needs ownership of\n * `configurations` and `configuration_history` — it does NOT need ownership of\n * the schema. Schema ownership additionally confers DROP SCHEMA and the right to\n * reassign everything inside it, which is exactly the authority the boundary\n * exists to withhold, and no part of the RLS path uses it.\n *\n * So the tables, types and sequences stay with `config_service` and the boot\n * path is untouched; only the schema returns to the owner role, with\n * `USAGE, CREATE` granted so the service can still create objects it needs.\n *\n * Skips cleanly when `config_schema_owner` does not exist — a database that has\n * not run stage 008 has no boundary to restore, and inventing the role here\n * would put ownership somewhere no grant points at.\n */\nexport class RestoreConfigSchemaOwnerBoundary1807400000000 implements MigrationInterface {\n  name = 'RestoreConfigSchemaOwnerBoundary1807400000000';\n\n  private readonly logger = new MigrationLogger(this.name);\n\n  public async up(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n    await pinSearchPath(qr, 'config');\n\n    // One statement per query() call. A single string carrying the DO block AND\n    // the SELECT is a MULTI-statement simple query: the driver returns one result\n    // per statement, TypeORM hands back the LAST one's `rows` — which for a DO\n    // block is absent — so `rows` arrived undefined and `rows[0]` threw, aborting\n    // the whole config migration chain (and with it every bootstrap-from-scratch\n    // service assertion).\n    await qr.query(`\n      DO $$\n      BEGIN\n        IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'config_schema_owner') THEN\n          ALTER SCHEMA config OWNER TO config_schema_owner;\n          -- The runtime role keeps everything it actually uses: USAGE to reach\n          -- the schema, CREATE because the boot-time RLS/audit helpers may add\n          -- objects. Neither confers DROP SCHEMA nor REASSIGN OWNED.\n          GRANT USAGE, CREATE ON SCHEMA config TO config_service;\n        END IF;\n      END $$\n    `);\n\n    const rows: Array<{ applied: boolean }> = await qr.query(\n      `SELECT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'config_schema_owner') AS applied`,\n    );\n\n    this.logger.log(\n      rows[0]?.applied\n        ? 'config schema ownership returned to config_schema_owner; tables stay with config_service for RLS.'\n        : 'config_schema_owner absent (pre-stage-008 database) — schema ownership left as found.',\n    );\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * BOTH facts, because each has its own failure mode: the schema must not be\n   * owned by the login role (the boundary), and the tables must still be owned\n   * by it (the RLS install). A future change that \"fixes\" one by breaking the\n   * other fails here rather than at a service's cold start.\n   */\n  public async postCondition(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<boolean> {\n    const rows: Array<{ ok: boolean }> = await qr.query(`\n      SELECT (\n        NOT EXISTS (\n          SELECT 1\n            FROM pg_namespace n\n            JOIN pg_roles r ON r.oid = n.nspowner\n           WHERE n.nspname = 'config'\n             AND r.rolname = 'config_service'\n             AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'config_schema_owner')\n        )\n        AND NOT EXISTS (\n          SELECT 1\n            FROM pg_class c\n            JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = c.relnamespace\n            JOIN pg_roles r ON r.oid = c.relowner\n           WHERE n.nspname = 'config'\n             AND c.relkind IN ('r', 'p')\n             AND c.relname IN ('configurations', 'configuration_history')\n             AND r.rolname <> 'config_service'\n        )\n      ) AS ok\n    `);\n    return rows[0]?.ok === true;\n  }\n\n  public async down(_qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n    this.logger.warn(\n      'Down intentionally a no-op: handing a schema back to a login role would ' +\n        're-open the privilege boundary this migration closed.',\n    );\n  }\n}\n","numLines":119,"startLine":1,"totalLines":119}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EnLdj2zmhHdudkWug1kCC6","type":"tool_result","content":"570\t    }\n571\t    if (drifts.length > 0) {\n572\t      throw new Error(\n573\t        `B.2 declared-vs-physical schema drift (${drifts.length}):\\n  ` + drifts.join('\\n  '),\n574\t      );\n575\t    }\n576\t  }, 30_000);\n577\t\n578\t  // B.4 — Per-service schema ownership at the role level.\n579\t  //\n580\t  // The security property is the SEPARATION, not merely that some role owns the\n581\t  // schema: DDL authority lives on a NOLOGIN `<svc>_schema_owner` role, while\n582\t  // the login role the service runs as (`<svc>_service`) holds DML only. A\n583\t  // compromised runtime credential therefore cannot drop or re-shape a table.\n584\t  // Bootstrap stage 008 (\"Least-Privilege Runtime Boundary\") establishes it:\n585\t  // `REASSIGN OWNED BY <runtime> TO <owner>` then `ALTER SCHEMA … OWNER TO\n586\t  // <owner>`.\n587\t  //\n588\t  // This assertion previously expected the schema to be owned by the RUNTIME\n589\t  // role and pointed at `00-init-schemas.sh` lines 89-128 — a file archived on\n590\t  // 2026-05-18, describing the pre-stage-008 model. It was never noticed because\n591\t  // the spec named `db-migration-check.yml` as its home but appeared only in\n592\t  // that workflow's `paths:` filters and was never executed by a step\n593\t  // (FARM-MEDIUM-303). Its remediation advice had become actively dangerous:\n594\t  // running the suggested `ALTER SCHEMA \"farm\" OWNER TO farm_service` would hand\n595\t  // DDL power back to the runtime login role and collapse the very boundary the\n596\t  // assertion claims to protect.\n597\t  //\n598\t  // The (schema, owner, runtime) triples are READ FROM stage 008 rather than\n599\t  // re-typed here. A hardcoded copy is what rotted the first time; parsing the\n600\t  // bootstrap means a schema added there is covered automatically and a role\n601\t  // renamed there cannot disagree with this gate.\n602\t  it.each(LEAST_PRIVILEGE_SPECS.map((s) => [s.schema_name, s.owner_role, s.runtime_role]))(\n603\t    'B.4 — schema \"%s\" is owned by \"%s\", not by the runtime role \"%s\"',\n604\t    async (schemaName, ownerRole, runtimeRole) => {\n605\t      const result = await db.query<{ owner: string | null }>(\n606\t        `SELECT pg_get_userbyid(nspowner) AS owner\n607\t       FROM pg_namespace\n608\t       WHERE nspname = $1`,\n609\t        [schemaName],\n610\t      );\n611\t      if (result.rows.length === 0) {\n612\t        throw new Error(\n613\t          `B.4 schema \"${schemaName}\" does not exist in pg_namespace. ` +\n614\t            `Bootstrap stage 003 creates it; verify aqua-db-migrate ran against this database.`,\n615\t        );\n616\t      }\n617\t      const owner = result.rows[0]?.owner;\n618\t      if (owner === runtimeRole) {\n619\t        throw new Error(\n620\t          `B.4 schema \"${schemaName}\" is owned by its RUNTIME role \"${runtimeRole}\". ` +\n621\t            `That gives the service's login credential DDL authority over its own schema, ` +\n622\t            `which is the least-privilege boundary bootstrap stage 008 exists to close. ` +\n623\t            `Re-run stage 008, or \\`ALTER SCHEMA \"${schemaName}\" OWNER TO ${ownerRole}\\`.`,\n624\t        );\n625\t      }\n626\t      if (owner !== ownerRole) {\n627\t        throw new Error(\n628\t          `B.4 schema \"${schemaName}\" is owned by \"${owner}\", expected \"${ownerRole}\". ` +\n629\t            `Bootstrap stage 008 assigns ownership to the NOLOGIN owner role; a schema ` +\n630\t            `created manually by a superuser keeps \\`postgres\\` as owner and never gets ` +\n631\t            `the boundary. Fix via \\`ALTER SCHEMA \"${schemaName}\" OWNER TO ${ownerRole}\\`.`,\n632\t        );\n633\t      }\n634\t    },\n635\t  );\n636\t\n637\t  // The owner roles must not be able to log in: the boundary is only real while\n638\t  // DDL authority is reachable through role membership (db_migrate) rather than\n639\t  // through a credential someone can present.\n640\t  it('B.4b — schema-owner roles are NOLOGIN', async () => {\n641\t    const owners = LEAST_PRIVILEGE_SPECS.map((s) => s.owner_role);\n642\t    const result = await db.query<{ rolname: string }>(\n643\t      `SELECT rolname FROM pg_catalog.pg_roles\n644\t        WHERE rolname = ANY($1::text[]) AND rolcanlogin`,"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9a4c96ab-bfa5-4654-b4ee-6068c2702ebb","uuid":"d76ddab4-b5a7-4832-8d2b-ae1f22bafcfc","timestamp":"2026-09-19T23:36:53.480Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-766ab32e8788/e2e/tests/integration/schema-invariants.spec.ts","content":"    }\n    if (drifts.length > 0) {\n      throw new Error(\n        `B.2 declared-vs-physical schema drift (${drifts.length}):\\n  ` + drifts.join('\\n  '),\n      );\n    }\n  }, 30_000);\n\n  // B.4 — Per-service schema ownership at the role level.\n  //\n  // The security property is the SEPARATION, not merely that some role owns the\n  // schema: DDL authority lives on a NOLOGIN `<svc>_schema_owner` role, while\n  // the login role the service runs as (`<svc>_service`) holds DML only. A\n  // compromised runtime credential therefore cannot drop or re-shape a table.\n  // Bootstrap stage 008 (\"Least-Privilege Runtime Boundary\") establishes it:\n  // `REASSIGN OWNED BY <runtime> TO <owner>` then `ALTER SCHEMA … OWNER TO\n  // <owner>`.\n  //\n  // This assertion previously expected the schema to be owned by the RUNTIME\n  // role and pointed at `00-init-schemas.sh` lines 89-128 — a file archived on\n  // 2026-05-18, describing the pre-stage-008 model. It was never noticed because\n  // the spec named `db-migration-check.yml` as its home but appeared only in\n  // that workflow's `paths:` filters and was never executed by a step\n  // (FARM-MEDIUM-303). Its remediation advice had become actively dangerous:\n  // running the suggested `ALTER SCHEMA \"farm\" OWNER TO farm_service` would hand\n  // DDL power back to the runtime login role and collapse the very boundary the\n  // assertion claims to protect.\n  //\n  // The (schema, owner, runtime) triples are READ FROM stage 008 rather than\n  // re-typed here. A hardcoded copy is what rotted the first time; parsing the\n  // bootstrap means a schema added there is covered automatically and a role\n  // renamed there cannot disagree with this gate.\n  it.each(LEAST_PRIVILEGE_SPECS.map((s) => [s.schema_name, s.owner_role, s.runtime_role]))(\n    'B.4 — schema \"%s\" is owned by \"%s\", not by the runtime role \"%s\"',\n    async (schemaName, ownerRole, runtimeRole) => {\n      const result = await db.query<{ owner: string | null }>(\n        `SELECT pg_get_userbyid(nspowner) AS owner\n       FROM pg_namespace\n       WHERE nspname = $1`,\n        [schemaName],\n      );\n      if (result.rows.length === 0) {\n        throw new Error(\n          `B.4 schema \"${schemaName}\" does not exist in pg_namespace. ` +\n            `Bootstrap stage 003 creates it; verify aqua-db-migrate ran against this database.`,\n        );\n      }\n      const owner = result.rows[0]?.owner;\n      if (owner === runtimeRole) {\n        throw new Error(\n          `B.4 schema \"${schemaName}\" is owned by its RUNTIME role \"${runtimeRole}\". ` +\n            `That gives the service's login credential DDL authority over its own schema, ` +\n            `which is the least-privilege boundary bootstrap stage 008 exists to close. ` +\n            `Re-run stage 008, or \\`ALTER SCHEMA \"${schemaName}\" OWNER TO ${ownerRole}\\`.`,\n        );\n      }\n      if (owner !== ownerRole) {\n        throw new Error(\n          `B.4 schema \"${schemaName}\" is owned by \"${owner}\", expected \"${ownerRole}\". ` +\n            `Bootstrap stage 008 assigns ownership to the NOLOGIN owner role; a schema ` +\n            `created manually by a superuser keeps \\`postgres\\` as owner and never gets ` +\n            `the boundary. Fix via \\`ALTER SCHEMA \"${schemaName}\" OWNER TO ${ownerRole}\\`.`,\n        );\n      }\n    },\n  );\n\n  // The owner roles must not be able to log in: the boundary is only real while\n  // DDL authority is reachable through role membership (db_migrate) rather than\n  // through a credential someone can present.\n  it('B.4b — schema-owner roles are NOLOGIN', async () => {\n    const owners = LEAST_PRIVILEGE_SPECS.map((s) => s.owner_role);\n    const result = await db.query<{ rolname: string }>(\n      `SELECT rolname FROM pg_catalog.pg_roles\n        WHERE rolname = ANY($1::text[]) AND rolcanlogin`,","numLines":75,"startLine":570,"totalLines":746}}}

not computable in this session.\",\n      \"confidence\": 0.92,\n      \"judgment_group_id\": \"AIR-aria-evidence-judge-766ab32e8788\",\n      \"rationale\": \"What the migration does: apps/config-service/src/database/migrations/1800100000000-OwnConfigTablesByConfigService.ts:19 declares OwnConfigTablesByConfigService1800100000000, whose up() runs one DO block that raises when the config_service role is absent (:37), then transfers ownership of the config schema (:40), the configurations/configuration_history tables (:51), two enum types (:69) and the sequences owned by those tables, scoped through JOIN pg_depend (:77, :90), to the runtime login role; postCondition (:103) re-queries pg_catalog and down() is a logged no-op (:163). Ownership transfers are privilege-boundary DDL with no rollback, and the repository's own chain records the harm this exact file caused: 1807400000000-RestoreConfigSchemaOwnerBoundary.ts:13-17 states that :40 reversed bootstrap stage 008's least-privilege boundary and that two gates in one deploy pipeline demanded opposite states. That is what makes the migration hazardous and the high severity reasonable. Test coverage: a repository-wide search for the class name and the 1800100000000 stamp returns no .spec.ts that imports this file. The adjacent directory apps/config-service/src/database/migrations/__tests__/ holds exactly one spec, 1807300000000-EnforcePermanentConfigurationErasure.spec.ts, which is the established convention for config-service migration tests (createMockDataSource at :1, import of its migration at :4, up() driven at :15, down() at :28) and which this migration lacks. The only spec that names an OwnConfigTablesByConfigService migration, tests/invariants/postgres-ddl-contract.spec.ts:41-65, resolves the archived path 1789100000000-OwnConfigTablesByConfigService.ts (:52); that path is absent from the live directory (the 1789100000000 file lives under .archive/), so the test returns via expect(true).toBe(true) at :54-57 and its sequence-scope assertions (:60-64) never execute against the live 1800100000000 file, whose :73-96 is precisely the ownership-repair path the test's comment (:42-48) believes no longer exists. tests/invariants/drift-repair-naming.spec.ts:88 is a filename allowlist entry for the same dead path, skipped at :131, and asserts nothing about behaviour. The one live-DB check touching config ownership, e2e/tests/integration/schema-invariants.spec.ts:602-635 (B.4), reads nspowner and FAILS if the schema is owned by config_service (:618), i.e. it asserts the state 1807400000000:60 establishes after undoing this migration's :40 — it verifies the override, not this migration. No spec under e2e/ or tests/ reads relowner for the table/type/sequence ownership this migration is responsible for, which is the ownership config-service's boot-time RLS install actually depends on (1807400000000:26-29). The runtime postCondition at 1807400000000:97-106 does check table ownership when the chain executes, but it is a migration, not a test, and cannot attribute the outcome to this file. apps/config-service/src/database/data-source.ts:32 only globs the file for execution. Conclusion: the finding's claim — no adjacent or importing test coverage signal for a hazardous migration — is accurate at ba4366830605ab4584de58169090cb6b8fde9027. Downstream surface if left uncovered: config-service cold start (RLS install needs table ownership), the stage-008 privilege boundary, and every from-scratch bootstrap in db-migration-check.yml. Recommended root-cause fix (Tier 3, make it detectable): add apps/config-service/src/database/migrations/__tests__/1800100000000-OwnConfigTablesByConfigService.spec.ts on the createMockDataSource pattern asserting the role guard text, that every OWNER TO target is config_service, that the table and sequence loops are scoped to configurations/configuration_history via JOIN pg_depend, that postCondition returns false when rows are returned and true when empty, and that down() emits the forward-only warning; and retarget tests/invariants/postgres-ddl-contract.spec.ts:52 to the live 1800100000000 path, removing the existsSync short-circuit so its sequence-scope assertion executes.\",\n      \"evidence_refs\": [\n
